## ⛔⭐⭐⭐⭐⭐ LIVE CURSOR — 2026-08-19 s172 (local seat `/home/claude5`: Claude Opus 5, queue row `beauty-m3-zls` = M1-R3) — **WALL-2 IS DOWN. BEAUTY-m3 NO LONGER SEGVs ON A REAL STATEMENT: IT NOW PRINTS, BYTE FOR BYTE, WHAT BEAUTY-m4 PRINTS. THE WALL WAS AN `EVAL` USE-AFTER-FREE, NOT A DIRTY QUAD — AND IT WAS GATED ON THE PROGRAM'S OWN CODE SIZE.**
**FINDING:** `FINDING-2026-08-19-s172-b2c-is-an-eval-retain-budget-use-after-free-not-a-dirty-quad.md`. **NEXT RUNG: M1-R2 `b1c-m4-seam` (row 4, seat6) is now the SINGLE wall on beauty's tiny input in BOTH modes**; M1-R4 `b1c-retreat` (row 5) landed at seat7 behind `SCRIP_CAP_NAME_STRICT`; M1-R5 `beauty-fixed-point` stays last.
**The mechanism.** An `EVAL` that returns a PATTERN hands back a value whose deferred elements live INSIDE the chain's own JIT blob. `eval_string_transient` ended with `if (mark < EVAL_RETAIN_BUDGET) eval_cache_put(...); else bb_pool_release(mark);` where `mark = bb_pool_mark()` is the whole pool's frontier — **the program's own compiled code included** — so the predicate asked *"is this program smaller than 2MB?"*, never *"how much has EVAL retained?"*. Beauty-m3's blobs seal at `0x202000` = 2,105,344 bytes, **8,192 bytes past the 2MB budget**, so EVERY beauty `EVAL` took the release arm: `bb_pool_release` mprotect'ed the blob back to RW and rewound `pool_top` over it, and `semantic.inc:16`'s `shift = EVAL("p . thx . *Shift('" t "', thx)")` was dangling before the statement ended. m4 escaped ONLY because its code lives in the ELF and not the pool (`mark` ≈ 0 ⇒ retained) — **the mode asymmetry was an accounting artifact.**
**The evidence.** gdb: `rip = rax = [DTP+0] = 0x7fffee202000`, `info proc mappings` shows `0x7fffee000000-0x7fffee202000 r-xp` (sealed slab) and `0x7fffee202000-… rw-p` — the faulting pc IS `pool_top`, page-aligned because `bb_alloc` page-aligns blob starts and unexecutable because the release un-sealed it. ⛔ **`#1 zls_g_region` IS NOT A CALLER** — it is an EMIT-TIME function whose stale return address sits in the driver's dirty stack; gdb renders that quad as frame #1. The s170 census read the corpse of an earlier call as the defect. **B2c's dirty-quad/carve-zeroing hypothesis is FALSIFIED as the explanation of beauty's m3 wall** (the dirty stack is real; it is what made the backtrace lie — no dirty quad was ever read).
**The fix (SCRIP, runtime only — 13 lines in `src/runtime/runtime_eval.c`, zero codegen).** `eval_retain_budget()`, house killswitch idiom: unset ⇒ retain always (what every sub-2MB program already got); `SCRIP_EVAL_RETAIN=2097152` restores the historic cliff VERBATIM; `=0` releases every chain. Dead `EVAL_RETAIN_BUDGET` macro deleted (ONE AUTHORITY). The `if (!fn) bb_pool_release(mark)` early arm STAYS — a chain that failed to build is referenced by nothing. Exhaustion now fails honestly: `bb_alloc` NULL at the 64MB reserve ⇒ the EVAL fails instead of jumping into freed pages.
**Measured, both arms, same binary:** beauty m3 `START` → `Parse Error` + raw echo rc=0 (default) vs **SIGSEGV rc=139** (`=2097152`) · beauty m3 `      X = 1` same shape · beauty **m4 unchanged** in both arms · `test_corpus_snobol4.sh` **byte-identical both arms** (m3 326/11, m4 322/13, SKIP 2) · `beauty_suite` m3 **17/17 both arms** · 7-line witness `corpus/probe/b2c/b2c_eval_pat_release.sno` oracle-identical at default, SIGSEGV at `=0`. `.s` blast radius is structurally zero (no emitter/template/lowerer file touched).
⛔ **INSTRUMENT LAW EARNED THE HARD WAY:** a `&`-backgrounded board script SURVIVED its tool call in this container, and a second copy appended to the SAME output files — minting 130 phantom "repairs" out of duplicate lines while both arms still reported equal totals. **Two writers on one board file is a FALSE BOARD.** Re-run with one process per arm and a fresh output dir; check `sort -u | wc -l` against `wc -l` before believing any set diff.

**FINDING:** `FINDING-2026-08-19-s170-b1c-retreat-the-capture-target-is-a-name-context-and-the-commit-flush-cannot-retreat.md`. Watermark SCRIP `97ad2912` · corpus `de42b605` · pristine · **RT_OPT=-O0** · oracle cloned · m3.

**Oracle law (14 ablations):** the right operand of `.`/`$` is a **NAME CONTEXT** — `*VAR` names **the variable itself** (`Q='q0'; 'A' . *Q` sets Q, leaves q0 null), a call supplies a name **only via NRETURN**, and a plain `RETURN` yields a value ⇒ the node **abandons the statement, anchor not advanced** (`'abc' ? EVAL("LEN(0) . *F()")` prints ONE `F called`). ⛔ The discriminator is **RETURN-vs-NRETURN, not the DESCR** — `F='ZZ'` returns the identical STRING either way and sbl matches one, retreats on the other.
**Root cause:** SCRIP read `. *E` as `. $E` (evaluate, spend the VALUE as an indirect name, always succeed). It could only `fprintf` a WARN.

## 2026-08-19 s169 (local seat `/home/claude2`: Claude Opus 5, queue row `gc-w1`) — **GC-W1 LANDED (SCRIP `b7e10d3c`): MARK IS NO LONGER A WHOLE-ARENA FIXPOINT. TITLES WALKED COLLAPSE **22,057,524 → 779** ON ONE `table_access` COLLECT (28,315×). ⛔ AND THE HONEST READ THAT RE-PRICES THE LADDER: THE WALL-CLOCK GAIN IS 0–10%, SO THE FIXPOINT WAS NEVER THE 835 ms STALL'S MASS.**

**NEXT RUNG: GC-W2 (walk-floor diet)** — now the ladder's biggest remaining lever, and the reason is measured, not assumed (below). Pairs with seat1's GC-R verdict in the cursor beneath this one: the cadence road is blocked, so the walk-floor road carries the weight.

**What landed.** The fixpoint (`while (changed) for (i = 0; i < g_gc_nblk; i++)`) is replaced by a mark stack seeded by the root passes; a block pushes the first time it is marked; `scanned[]`/`changed` are deleted. The worklist is **INTRUSIVE — it costs zero storage**: the link rides `h->fwd`, which the index pass already zeroes and which nothing reads until the forwarding pass, so push is `h->fwd = head; head = h` and pop restores `fwd = 0`, leaving the arena exactly as ADJUST expects. All six mark sites (`rt_gc_pin_ptr`, `gc_cons_scan_t` ws-only, `rt_gc_visit_raw`, `gc_mark_agg`, `rt_gc_visit_descr` DT_S and DT_N) now route through ONE choke point, `gc_mark_blk()`, which pushes only scannable types (WS/PLJ/AGG*).

⛔ **THIS RUNG REMOVES A GLOBAL RATHER THAN ADDING ONE.** `g_gc_scanbuf` + `g_gc_scap` existed only to serve the fixpoint and retire with it; one head pointer replaces both — **net −1 file-scope global**, so the FACT RULE needed no banner ask. The killswitch arm allocates its `scanned[]` locally per collect.

**Killswitch:** `SCRIP_GC_WORKLIST=0` restores the fixpoint verbatim (draining the pushed list first so `fwd` is clean). Default ON. New `[ZGC-MARK]` telemetry under `SCRIP_ZETA_TELEM` prints arm · titles-walked · blocks-scanned · rounds · nblk.

**⭐ THE FINDING THAT MATTERS MORE THAN THE RUNG — GC-W1 DOES NOT CLOSE s154, AND NOW WE KNOW WHY.** The count collapse is enormous and the wall-clock gain is not: `table_access` 771 → **729 ms**, `table_churn` 820 → **739 ms**, `array_sum` 609 → **608 ms** (best-of-3, RT_OPT `-O0` dev default, default heap). Each title the fixpoint re-visited cost only a flags test and a `continue`, so **22 M skipped visits buy ~40 ms** — the fixpoint was **~5–10% of GC wall, not the bulk**. s167's divergence #1 is real and is now fixed, but the 835 ms stall's mass lives in divergences #2 (the five unconditional O(all-blocks) passes → **GC-W2**) and #3 (every survivor pinned → **GC-P**). **Any plan that expected GC-W1 to be the s154 fix should be re-cut.**

**Gates (all green; pristine tree at `25d8970c` plus this change):** `test_gc_stress_suite.sh` **ALL GREEN** (4 stress rungs × 2 modes × 15 tests, incl. `SCRIP_GC_STRESS=1` collect-every-alloc) · corpus both modes **307/306 PASS, 10 FAIL, 0 DIVERGE**, and the 10 reds are **identical program-for-program with the killswitch OFF**, so they are pre-existing · Icon smoke **14/14 both modes** · Prolog smoke 3/5 (= the s165 watermark, arm-independent) · **`.s` blast 0** (484 crosscheck artifacts re-emitted byte-identical — runtime-`.so` only, as D-22 requires).

**Full record:** `FINDING-2026-08-19-s169-gc-w1-worklist-mark-and-the-fixpoint-was-never-the-stall.md`.

## ⛔⭐⭐⭐⭐⭐ LIVE CURSOR — 2026-08-19 s169 (seat1, Opus 5) — **GC-R MEASURED AND ANSWERED: THE BUDGET KNOB CANNOT PRICE THE CADENCE HYPOTHESIS. VERDICT IS NO, AND THE ROAD IS BLOCKED BEHIND GC-W1 + GC-P1.**
**FINDING:** `FINDING-2026-08-19-s169-gc-r-the-budget-knob-cannot-price-the-cadence-hypothesis.md`. Pristine `a78b39fb`, **RT_OPT=-O0**, `SCRIP_NOHUGE=1`, m3, median of 5.
**Three measured reasons, each independently sufficient.** (1) **CONFOUNDED BY CONSTRUCTION:** setting `SCRIP_GC_BUDGET_MB` forces `g_alloc_detax=-1` and disarms `g_hp_fr.armed`, the INLINE ALLOCATION FAST PATH (gc_heap.c:219–220) — priced with a `SCRIP_GC_STRESS=<huge>` control that disarms the detax while never collecting: **−10% to −14% throughput on rows where gc=0 in BOTH arms**, i.e. before cadence exists. `STRESS-huge` and `BUDGET4` give byte-identical medians on `string_pattern`/`string_manip`, confirming the arms differ in nothing but the allocator. (2) **STRUCTURALLY GATED:** the trigger is AND-ed with "arena ≥ half full", so at the shipping `SCRIP_HEAP_MB=512` it cannot fire before **256 MB** is allocated — the knob controls cadence only inside the top half of an arena sized elsewhere. (3) **THE HYPOTHESIS CANNOT PAY OFF YET:** the mechanism IS real (heap sweep on `table_access`: 24 collections at 16 MB beat 1 at 256 MB — each collect is O(every block ever allocated), so a smaller arena makes it cheaper), and inside a small heap the knob does win (HEAP=64: BASE 1792 gc5 → BUDGET4 3584 gc135, 2.0×, and *steadier*, ±3.6% vs ±12.5%) — **but the best cadence arm still LOSES to not collecting at all: 2944 (HEAP=64 BUDGET4, gc115) vs 3840 (HEAP=1024 BASE, gc0).** Every cadence win is relative to a small heap, and the small heap is itself the loss. Cause is divergence 3: **every survivor is pinned, so a collection reclaims nothing** — more collections buy only the O(heap) saving from a smaller arena and pay full mark + 5-pass floor + compaction bookkeeping for zero reclaim. SPITBOL's small-often works because its regions genuinely compact.
**Knob helped in 1 of 10 measured cells** (`table_access` @ HEAP=64); neutral or harmful in the other nine (`array_sum` @512: 4608 → 2560, −44%). ⛔ **THE D-22 ORDERING IS LOAD-BEARING, NOT CONVENIENT:** GC-R is not merely unproven before GC-W1 (mark → O(live edges)) and GC-P1 (de-foreign the aggregates so `pz` fires and a collect actually returns memory) — it is **UNTESTABLE**. The two rows where cadence mattered at all, `table_access` and `array_sum`, are exactly the TABLE/ARRAY programs s167 found structurally locked out of the precise mode (`nforeign>0 ⇒ pz=0 ⇒ conservative forever`). **NEXT ON THIS LADDER: GC-W1 (queue row 4), then GC-P1; re-run GC-R after both, keeping the detax control arm.**
**Routed for HQ (no code touched this rung):** do NOT expose `SCRIP_GC_BUDGET_MB` as a tuning knob; if revived, **decouple the cadence trigger from `g_alloc_detax` first** (they share a predicate for no reason) and treat the arena-half-full precondition as a defect, not policy. Production guidance now measured: raising `SCRIP_HEAP_MB` past the working set beats every cadence arm — the timed runner's `HEAP=1024` default is correct and should stay.

## ⛔⭐⭐⭐⭐⭐ LIVE CURSOR — 2026-08-19 s169 (seat3 `/home/claude3`: Claude Opus 5, PT front — queue row 6 `pt-json`, rung PT-J) — **json HAS NO PERFORMANCE CURVE. IT HAS TWO WALLS, AND ">60 s" WAS NEVER THE PROBLEM — IT IS NON-TERMINATION.**
SCRIP `a78b39fb` (read-only this rung) · corpus `a3604cc9` · .github this commit. Record: `FINDING-2026-08-19-s169-ptj-json-has-no-curve-it-has-two-walls.md`. **Instrument-only per the PT charter — NO fixes, no codegen touched, so no `.s` regens owed.** All numbers RT_OPT=`-O0`.

### THE VERDICT PT-J ASKED FOR — A NEGATIVE ONE, AND IT IS STRONGER THAN A SLOPE
- **⛔ THE 631K→64K→8K→1K BISECT CANNOT BE RUN, AND THAT IS THE FINDING.** json's cost is not a function of input SIZE — it is a step function of input SHAPE, and both steps sit three orders of magnitude below the smallest rung the brief names. **Neither linear nor superlinear: there is no curve.**
- **⛔⭐⭐⭐ WALL 1 — THE FIRST `ARBNO` ITERATION OVER A DEFERRED (`*`) BODY NEVER RETURNS.** `[]` · `["s"]` · `{"a":["s"]}` (zero ARBNO iterations) all complete in ~1.1 s. `["s","s"]` · `{"a":"s","b":"s"}` · `{"a":["s","s"]}` (ONE iteration) never complete. Controlled ladder `{"a":["s" × n]}` puts the wall between **n=1 (12 B, 1 ms)** and **n=2 (17 B, no completion)**; every row is **5 of 5 clean runs**. ⛔ **SCOPE IT:** this is `json.sno`'s CAPTURING grammar — the pure-grammar sibling `json-match.sno` matches `["s","s"]` fine and fails differently (SIGSEGV on any non-empty object). Reduction to a small witness FAILED this seat; `probe/ptj/ptj1` carries the whole grammar and shows both halves in one run. **It is a control-flow loop, not allocation growth: still spinning at 4 min 11 s with RSS FLAT at 28 MB; 420 s budget, no completion.** Every real JSON document has 2+ members somewhere, so the benchmark never runs at ANY size.
- **⛔⭐⭐ WALL 2 — ANY NUMBER WITH A LEADING DIGIT 1–9 SIGSEGVs.** `0`, `0.5`, `0.087` fine; `1` `2` `9` `12` `123` `10` `100` `-1` `1.5` `1e5` `1.5e-3` all SIGSEGV. The grammar splits exactly there: `('0' | ANY('123456789') (SPAN('0123456789') | ''))` — the `'0'` arm is safe, **the `ANY(...) (SPAN(...) | '')` arm faults.**
- **`json-match.sno` (the PURE-MATCH sibling, the one PT most wants) IS WORSE:** `{}` ok, but `{"a":[]}` · `{"a":"s"}` · `{"a":1}` all SIGSEGV, and it SIGSEGVs at **every rung** of the 409 B→631 KB ladder.
- **⭐ ONE MINIMAL WITNESS, AND IT IS A WRONG ANSWER NOT A CRASH:** `elem = *val` / `val = ('"s"' | '[' elem ARBNO(',' elem) ']')` on `["s","s"]` — **SCRIP prints FAIL, SPITBOL prints MATCH**, 9 ms, no crash. Self-recursive and non-recursive spellings both match correctly. **This is json's exact shape** — `json-match.sno` defines `jmember`/`jarray` against `*jelement` while `jelement` is assigned LAST.
- **⭐ WITNESSES MINTED — `corpus/probe/ptj/`, oracle-ref'd, every one VERIFIED TO ACTUALLY REPRODUCE.** ⛔ My first `ptj1` and `ptj2` were WRONG (self-recursive spelling / lifted-out `jnumber`) and both matched correctly — witnesses that witnessed nothing. Caught only by running them; rebuilt against the real grammars. **A probe that does not reproduce retires a defect on paper — run every witness you mint.**
- **⛔ ALL OF IT PREDATES seat3's OWN FZ-3 COMMIT — PROVEN, NOT ASSUMED.** Identical on a pristine build of `f0ae498d` (the parent of `a78b39fb`). `json-match.sno` has ZERO fences and FZ-3 ships disarmed. The oracle is correct and 0 ms on every one of these.

### ⛔⭐⭐ NEXT SEAT — PICK UP EXACTLY HERE
1. **STRIKE json FROM PT'S MEASUREMENT SET until the three defects land** — it is not a performance workload today, it is three correctness defects wearing a stopwatch. **treebank remains the workhorse; PT-0/PT-1/PT-2/PT-3 are unaffected and still unrun.**
2. **REPAIR RANK BY BLAST RADIUS:** (a) ARBNO-first-iteration-over-deferred-body non-termination — the one that makes the benchmark unrunnable and the likeliest to be hurting the real grammar corpus; (b) the `ANY(...) (SPAN(...) | '')` SIGSEGV; (c) the forward-deferred-reference wrong answer. **⛔ (a) and (c) may be one defect — do not assume they are two.**
3. **A THIRD, SMALLER OBSERVATION, HONESTLY BOUNDED:** `{"a":"s"}` under the stock harness exhausts the 512 MB arena on **4 of 5 clean runs** (intermittent, shape-sensitive) — but it is NOT simple per-iteration leakage: 400 separate `ZBODY(1)` calls AND a single `ZBODY(10000)` both complete cleanly. Whatever accumulates is in the harness calibrate/measure path.
4. **⛔ MEASUREMENT HYGIENE, RECORDED BECAUSE IT BIT ME:** my first construct table ran while a 420 s background job held a core and reported HEAP rows the clean re-run does not reproduce. **It is retracted in the FINDING.** A benchmark measured under concurrent load is a measurement of the load — `ps` for stragglers before every timing table on this front.

## 2026-08-19 s167 (HQ, Fable 5) — **GC: THE REAL REASON WE LOSE TO SPITBOL, READ FROM SOURCE (Lon's question: "we both have MARK, ADJUST, SLIDE — find the real reason"). SAME THREE PHASES, THREE READ-CONFIRMED DIVERGENCES IN THE COST MODEL. RUNG LADDER MINTED, DELEGATED AS D-22.**

**Vocabulary (Lon asked):** SURVIVORS = blocks still reachable when a collect fires. GARBAGE = blocks allocated but no longer reachable. An **O(survivors)** collector's work scales with LIVE data only — mark follows pointers from roots (dead blocks are never visited), and the region it slides holds only last-collect's survivors plus what was allocated since. An **O(heap)** collector's work scales with EVERY block ever allocated. s154 measured ours squarely in the second class: **7,352,520 titles walked to keep 1,548 live (0.02%), 835 ms, and total GC cost invariant across heap sizes (2811/2657/3297 ms = 65–70% of wall)** — the invariance is the signature: each allocated block gets its walk visits no matter when the collect fires.

**The three divergences (source-read at `gc_heap.c`, SCRIP `917b4f45`):**
1. **MARK's completion is a whole-arena FIXPOINT, not a worklist** (`gc_collect_ex`, gc_heap.c:660–673): `while (changed) for (i = 0; i < g_gc_nblk; i++) …` — every round is a full pass over EVERY title in the arena asking "marked-and-unscanned?", and the round count grows with the reference-chain depth discovered round by round. SIL/SPITBOL mark FOLLOWS pointers from roots and never touches a dead block. Nuance kept honest: our precise root visitor `rt_gc_visit_descr` (:462) DOES recurse properly through arrays/tables/records — the fixpoint exists to finish the aggregate/WS/conservative classes, but it drags all 7.35M titles per round to find the few that need work.
2. **A fixed floor of ~5 unconditional O(all-blocks) passes per collect**: the count walk (:630) · flag-clear + index build + 512-byte pmap rebuild (:634–636) · the forwarding pass (:676) · the live-array pass (:691) — plus the fixpoint's ≥2 rounds. SPITBOL pays its linear region walk over a region that is TINY because of divergence 3 and its cadence.
3. **EVERY survivor was PINNED** (s154 telemetry: `pinned 1548` = nlive): conservative roots (C stack, ζ chain, registers — the price of compiling to native x86) set HBF_PIN, fwd=self, slide moves NOTHING, `g_hp_top` never drops (`bytes 536870848->536870848 reclaimed 0`), and allocation continues from the largest inter-pin WINDOW. SIL/SPITBOL has ZERO pins — every pointer lives in an adjustable descriptor, so the region truly compacts to survivors-only (oracle: 2.8 MB RSS, flat 30 ms batches, collections invisible). Our compactor degenerates toward a non-moving collector that still pays full compaction bookkeeping.
**Plus the trigger:** exhaustion at `SCRIP_HEAP_MB` (default 512) ⇒ one enormous stall instead of many tiny ones. **A budget trigger ALREADY EXISTS, unmeasured: `SCRIP_GC_BUDGET_MB` (gc_heap.c:218–222).**

### D-22 — GC-REGEN rung ladder (delegated; instrument-first; every rung killswitched, OFF-arm byte-identical; runtime-.so only ⇒ corpus `.s` blast must be 0)
- **GC-W1 — WORKLIST MARK: ✅ LANDED s169** (SCRIP `b7e10d3c`, net −1 global). Mark is O(live edges); titles walked 22,057,524 → 779 on one `table_access` collect. **But the wall-clock gain is 0–10% — the fixpoint was ~5–10% of GC wall, not the 835 ms stall's mass.** All gates green, `.s` blast 0. See the s169 LIVE CURSOR at the top of this file. **⇒ GC-W2 is now the biggest remaining lever, on measurement.**
- **GC-W2 — WALK-FLOOR DIET:** fold the five unconditional passes (index once, pmap incremental, forwarding+live-array fused). Gate: a per-collect pass census printed under `SCRIP_ZETA_TELEM`, before/after.
- **GC-R — CADENCE: ✅ DONE s169 (seat1), VERDICT NO.** A/B'd `SCRIP_GC_BUDGET_MB` small-vs-exhaustion on the timed family. The knob is confounded (it disarms the inline-alloc fast path, −10..−14% before any cadence change), structurally gated (cannot fire below a half-full arena ⇒ 256 MB at the shipping default), and the hypothesis cannot pay off while every survivor is pinned — the best cadence arm (2944) still loses to GC-free (3840). Helped in 1 of 10 cells. **Untestable until GC-W1 + GC-P1; re-run after.** See the s169 LIVE CURSOR above and `FINDING-2026-08-19-s169-gc-r-the-budget-knob-cannot-price-the-cadence-hypothesis.md`.
- **GC-P — PIN PRECISION, expanded s167-2 (Lon: "Can this problem of pinning be fixed?" — YES, three roads, and a DISCOVERY: ⭐ THE PRECISE MODE ALREADY EXISTS.** `gc_collect_ex` has a `pz` arm (gc_heap.c:638) that skips the conservative stack scan AND the class pins entirely (:677 pins only `if (!pz…)`) — no pins, everything marked MOVES, true compaction. It is reachable only through the `cons_stack=0` pending-drain entry (:382; `rt_gc_collect()` :706 hardwires `cons_stack=1`) and only when `nforeign==0` (zero ZBLK/PLJ/AGG blocks in the arena, :635) + no active scan + no coexpr + no value-trail + no reg-pins. The s154 benchmark pinned 1548/1548 because TABLEs are AGG blocks ⇒ `nforeign>0` ⇒ `pz=0` ⇒ conservative forever — **the allocating benchmarks are structurally locked out of the precise mode that already exists.**)
  - **GC-P0 — COLLECT AT BOUNDARIES:** exhaustion sets `g_gc_pending` and satisfies the triggering allocation from a reserve; the collect runs at the next boundary drain (:382 machinery exists) where the C stack is shallow and `rt_scan_active()` is false. Shrinks the conservative root set even when pz can't fire. Cheap; measure pin counts before/after via `[ZGC]` pin-classes telemetry (:685 exists).
  - **GC-P1 — DE-FOREIGN THE AGGREGATES (the pz unlock):** ARBLK/TBBLK/DATINST/VCELL are ALREADY precisely traced by `rt_gc_visit_descr` (:462) — what keeps them pinned/foreign is raw C interior pointers (TBPAIR chains, cell pointers) that ADJUST cannot follow. Their layouts are known structs: teach adjust to rewrite those interior pointers (same dedup-hash discipline, :342), reclassify them out of `nforeign` ⇒ pz fires for table/array programs ⇒ the exact benchmark losers (table_access 0.48x, array_sum) get compaction. Family-by-family ladder, one killswitch each.
  - **GC-P2 — ZB-4/TR-5 FRAME + REGISTER MAPS (full SIL-grade precision):** the compiler KNOWS every ζ-frame layout at emit time (zd_plan) — emit per-frame layout tables so the ζ chain marks AND adjusts precisely even mid-match (scan_stack already restores-from-memory at scan-leave and is adjustable — the precedent, :337); register maps at call sites finish it. Everything moves, always — SIL's guarantee achieved the modern-compiler way. Design note first; Lon prices.
  - **ACCOUNTABILITY (routed s167-2):** the pin degeneration was documented only as code comments ("conservative-pin is the sound v1 until ZB-4 emits layouts") and one telemetry token (`pinned 1548`) — never as a headline to Lon. HQ failure class: a "sound v1" that silently disables a milestone-relevant property (compaction) is a FINDING, not a comment.
Sequencing: GC-W1 is the s154 defect's actual owner; GC-W2 removes the floor; GC-R is a same-day measurement; GC-P0/P1 unlock real compaction for the allocating class; GC-P2 is the horizon. **PT (D-21) stays independent** — it measures where PATTERN time goes; do not merge the seats.

**Also this slice — BOEHM SWEEP EXECUTED (Lon's order):** every libgc/Boehm reference removed from SCRIP (26 sites, SCRIP `917b4f45`). ## ⛔⭐⭐⭐⭐⭐ LIVE CURSOR — 2026-08-19 s168 (seat3 `/home/claude3`: Claude Opus 5, FENCE front — queue row 9 `fz-3`) — **FZ-3 LANDED: THE CUT IS VISIBLE TO THE DEPTH PLANNER, AND THERE WERE TWO DEPTH SPELLINGS TO TELL, NOT ONE. THE ARM IS WITNESS-CLEAN AND STILL SHIPS DEFAULT OFF — BY DECISION, NOT BY DEFECT.**
SCRIP `a78b39fb` · corpus `a3604cc9` · .github this commit. Record: `FINDING-2026-08-19-s168-fz3-the-cut-is-now-visible-to-the-depth-planner.md`.
⛔ CODEGEN TOUCHED (`emit.cpp`, `bb_match_fence0.cpp`) — `.s` regens **deliberately NOT run per HQ-48**: queue row 2 `regen-catchup` owns that sweep as its own commit.

### WHAT LANDED
- **FZ-3 — a cut is a node of depth contribution `K-REL`.** `zd_plan` now subtracts the release from the SAME accumulator K is added to (`zout[i] = zd + K - REL`), so every `[rsp+off]` right of the cut is staged at the post-release depth. Everything downstream follows for free — operand reads (`zd_out[reader] - zd_out[producer]`) shrink by REL exactly as RSP rose by REL, and `zgpop`/`zwpop` pop the remainder instead of double-popping what the cut freed. **β needs no special case:** the released cells are exactly the ones whose β the cut skips. The emitted change on the s166 witness is ONE line, `[rsp+32]` → `[rsp+16]`.
- **⭐ A SECOND SITE OF THE IDENTICAL DEFECT, and only a NEW witness found it.** `op_zpat` — an independent sum of the pattern's ζ cells feeding `bb_match_replace`'s non-rbp cursor road — also brackets the cut. Under `SCRIP_MATCH_RBP=0` it read **48 in BOTH arms while RSP moved**, and `fz7_replace_across_cut` printed `R=Zaaabbbccc` for the oracle's `R=Zccc`. Threading the accumulator alone would have shipped a rung still wrong on a reachable road. **"The depth model" was not one thing.**
- **ONE AUTHORITY on the arm.** `fence0_whack_on()` MOVED from `bb_match_fence0.cpp` into `emit.cpp`; the gate now lives inside `fence0_release_bytes()`, which answers "how many bytes does this cut actually spend" for BOTH customers. The template's copy is DELETED, not duplicated — a template-side gate is invisible to the planner, and an armed planner meeting a disarmed template skews every staged offset by exactly the release. **Net new global state: zero.** No new `IR_t` field.
- **Five new witnesses + three new gate locks.** `corpus/probe/fz/` grows to 8 (`fz4_group_capture_chain`, `fz5_two_cuts_one_group`, `fz6_cut_on_fail_path`, `fz7_replace_across_cut`, `fz8_arbno_after_cut`; refs from live `sbl`). `test_gate_fz_release.sh` grows 2 locks → 5: **LOCK 3** armed-vs-oracle (replacing s166's non-verdict REPORT), **LOCK 4** the release actually rebases staged offsets and moves nothing else (this is the lock that would have caught FZ-3 — at s166 it reads *releases 80B, 0 offsets rebased*), **LOCK 5** the `op_zpat` road. All three proven **NON-VACUOUS: RED on the pre-fix baseline binary, GREEN here.**
- **MEASURED:** disarmed inertness 510 programs patched-vs-baseline **509 identical + `unary_not.sno` re-proved pre-existing-nondeterministic (6 runs → 6 md5s, zero fence nodes) ⇒ 0 attributable movers** · crosscheck m3 **307/10** · m4 **306/10** · **DIVERGE=0** FAIL sets identical in both arms · `treebank-match-fence` **check: 100155** both arms · 8/8 witnesses green ARMED and DISARMED, m3+m4. All re-proven after the rebase onto `f0ae498d`.

### ⛔⭐⭐ NEXT SEAT — PICK UP EXACTLY HERE
1. **⛔⭐⭐⭐ THE ARMING DECISION IS THE OPEN ITEM, AND IT IS HQ'S/LON'S — NOT A SEAT'S.** Asked as `q-fz3-arming`; unanswered at handoff. FZ-3 was the named blocker and it is CLEARED, so `DEFAULT OFF` is no longer a defect statement — it is a decision awaiting a ruling. **The case FOR:** 8 witnesses green both arms both modes, over-release locked (LOCK 2), both depth spellings threaded, the count re-derived from emitted asm with no golden pinned. **The case AGAINST:** the corpus contributes *exactly nothing* — armed ≡ disarmed on all 510 programs because the arm emits no release anywhere in it — and this class already cored the tree once (s154 floor whack), with a failure mode of silent over-release into live storage. If the ruling is to flip, it is **its own attributable commit**, not a fold-in.
2. **⛔ THE CORPUS STILL CANNOT CERTIFY THIS FEATURE — unchanged from s166 §3 and re-measured here.** Every corpus fence is `FENCE(P)`, first-position, or inside an ARBNO/blob body with no bracket on its forward chain. Any future FENCE0 verdict rides `corpus/probe/fz/` witnesses (HQ-48: *"never corpus boards"*). **Widening the witness set is what found the second site — do that before trusting any new FENCE0 claim.**

## LIVE CURSOR — 2026-08-19 s153 (web seat: Claude Opus 5, CN front) — **g-cn2 PAID, AND IT CAUGHT A LIVE T1 DEFECT: THE FOLD LEAKED INTO EVAL/CODE FRAGMENTS AND `EVAL('&N')` RETURNED **EMPTY** IN m3 WHILE m4 RETURNED 42 — A MODE34-IDENTICAL VIOLATION ON THE DEFAULT ARM AND A SILENT WRONG ANSWER. REPAIRED IN ONE LINE; THE NEW GATE IS PROVEN TO GO RED WITHOUT IT.**
SCRIP `b69c63a5`+g-cn2 · corpus `d1ab283b`+2 witnesses. Record: `FINDING-2026-08-19-s153-g-cn2-t1-fold-leaked-into-eval-fragments.md`.

### ⛔ THE DEFECT — WHAT THE OWED WITNESS FOUND
`&USER_DECLARED_CONSTANTS = 1` · `&N = 42` · `EVAL('&N')`, pristine at `b69c63a5`: **m3/T1-on = EMPTY · m3/T1-off = 42 · m4/both = 42.** `EVAL('&N + 1')` returned **`1`, not `43`** — the empty read coerced to 0 and the statement completed. No failure, no abort, no diagnostic. Confound ruled out FIRST: `EVAL('N')`→42, `EVAL('19')`→19, `EVAL('3 * 4')`→12, `EVAL('&ANCHOR')`→0 — EVAL is healthy; only tier-3 user constants broke.
**Root cause:** `g_sno_seal_enabled` carries the doc *"the runtime EVAL/CODE fragment compiler … must stay conservative"* since s137 — and **nothing ever cleared it**. `lower_sno_stage2` grants `=1` at `:2590` and there is no other write; `sno_lower_fragment_at` scrubs the FREEZE tables (`g_sno_nfz`/`g_sno_fz_unsafe`/`g_sno_nsnapref`/`g_sno_nencl`) and leaves the SEAL table and its gate live. A fragment therefore re-entered `sx_lower` holding the whole main program's seal table and folded `&N` against a `tree_t *` owned by a finished compile. **m4 was accidentally correct**: it compiles fragments in a DIFFERENT PROCESS whose table is empty, so it fell through to the sealed cell — the right route by luck. The repair makes m3 do deliberately what m4 was already doing.
**Why every prior gate was green AND honest:** the `0/529` killswitch and blast-radius numbers measure emitted `.s`; fragments compile at RUNTIME and reach no `.s` file. The instrument was sound and simply blind to this path — the s149 `CLEAR()`-is-a-noop shape exactly.

### THE REPAIR (one line, `sno_lower_fragment_at`)
`int seal_sv = g_sno_seal_enabled; g_sno_seal_enabled = 0;` … `g_sno_seal_enabled = seal_sv;` beside the freeze-table resets. It is ARCH-SN4-CONSTANTS's own ruling (*"no fold in the runtime-compile path is the safe default"*) made mechanical, and it **closes T1 and T2 together** — both `sno_const_val` and `sno_const_pat` gate on this one flag, and an inlined T2 graph carries the identical cross-compile pointer hazard. Fragments keep reading the SEALED CELL by name, which is what makes 341/342 answerable inside a thunk (CN-2's binding is process-global; the lowerer's table is not). Placed after both early `return NULL`s: one clear, one restore, no early-return leak. **Shared-entry safety CHECKED, not assumed:** `sno_lower_fragment_at`'s only external callers are `runtime_eval.c:451` and `lower_snobol4()`, and `lower_snobol4()` is itself called ONLY from `runtime_eval.c:286` — both runtime fragment compiles. The whole-program path enters at `lower_sno_stage2` and never routes through it. NO NEW GLOBAL (one function-local).

### GATES — AND THE GATE IS PROVEN NON-VACUOUS
`test_gate_udc.sh` **19/19** (was 12/12; +7 = `cn_t1_eval` × 2 T1 arms × 2 media + 3 on the abort lane). ⭐ **REVERT-PROBE: with the one line removed and the tree rebuilt, the gate reads 18/19 with exactly ONE red — `m3 cn_t1_eval CONST_T1=1`, precisely the one broken cell of the four and no other.** A gate that cannot go red proves nothing (s68); this one was made to go red before it was trusted. `test_gate_kw_static.sh --armed` **unchanged at 10/14** — only `kw_bare_shadow` (B1) and `kw_protected_write` (KW-5), the two already-routed non-regressions. cn suite **20/20** both media.
**Blast radius, A/B against a SEPARATE WORKTREE built at origin `b69c63a5`** (not this seat's tree — the s149 standing law; per-tree objdir made it cheap): `.s` md5 **0 movers / 527 comparable**, and — because the `.s` sweep structurally cannot see this path — **all 29 corpus EVAL/CODE programs byte-identical in m3, base vs fixed**. Regens ×3 run.

### ⛔⭐⭐ SECOND RULING REQUEST FOR LON — THE `$()` INDIRECT SEAL IS ASYMMETRIC (measured, with an oracle receipt)
Chasing s148's open note that `NV_EXISTS_fn` (`keywords.c:360`) is the wrong 342 predicate, the larger hole is that the predicate **is not reached at all** through aliasing. Truth table, measured at HEAD:

| via | construct | result |
|---|---|---|
| direct read, declared | `&N` | `42` ✓ |
| **indirect** read, declared | `$('&N')` | `42` ✓ — same cell, CN-2's canonical `"&Name"` key working as designed |
| **indirect** WRITE, sealed | `$('&N') = 99` | **341** ✓ — the seal holds through the alias |
| direct read, UNdeclared | `&NEVERSET` | **342** ✓ |
| **indirect** read, UNdeclared | `$('&NEVERSET')` | **`""` SILENT — no 342** ⛔ |

ARCH-SN4-CONSTANTS states *"No bypass via `OPSYN`/`$('&X')`/aliasing — the seal lives on the CELL"*. **That claim holds for writes and fails for the read diagnostic**, because 342 is spelled only in `rt_keyword_read_snobol4`'s tier-3 arm and the indirect path never traverses it. **⛔ THIS IS A RULING REQUEST, NOT A BUG REPORT, and I did not "fix" it** — because the two halves diverge from the oracle in opposite directions and only Lon can price that: **oracle receipt — stock `sbl` prints `x[]` for `$("&NEVERSET")`**, an ordinary null variable, so TODAY'S READ behaviour is ORACLE-TRUE, while the 341-on-indirect-write is already a DELIBERATE oracle divergence (CN-2's cell seal; stock `sbl` would simply assign). Ruling needed: **(a)** seal-on-the-cell wins ⇒ teach the indirect read path the 342 predicate, accepting a second oracle divergence; or **(b)** oracle fidelity wins for reads ⇒ `$()` is the documented escape hatch, and ARCH-SN4-CONSTANTS's "no bypass" sentence must be narrowed to WRITES in the design of record. Either way the two halves should agree; today they do not. Pinned in `probe/cn/cn_indirect_seal.{sno,ref}` so the ruling lands as a visible gate event. **NOTE for whoever takes it:** the s148 `NV_EXISTS_fn` note (predicate tests entry EXISTENCE, not ASSIGNMENT) is a REAL but SEPARATE defect one layer below this one — fixing the predicate does nothing for a path that never calls it.

### ⭐ SELF-CORRECTION, MEASURED — T2 WAS **NOT** BROKEN AT THIS BOUNDARY (the fix's T2 half is PROPHYLACTIC)
This seat's first write-up said the line "closes T1 and T2 together", which reads as *both were broken*. **Revert-probe says otherwise and the record is corrected:** a T2 witness (`&Word = SPAN(…)` · `EVAL('DATATYPE(&Word)')` · `P = EVAL('&Word')` then matched) reads `PATTERN`/`zebra` **identically with the fix and without it**. Cause is REACHABILITY, not luck: T2 folds at `*&Name` defer sites, and **`EVAL('*&Word')` raises Error 22 "Undefined function called"** — the deferred-expression construct is outside the fragment-compilable subset. A bare `EVAL('&Word')` is neither a T2 site nor a T1 site (T1 admits only ILIT/FLIT/QLIT), so it goes by-name to the sealed cell and is correct on both arms. **⛔ CONSEQUENCE FOR THE NEXT SEAT: the T2 half becomes load-bearing the instant the fragment subset widens to admit `*&Name`** — a fragment-subset rung would do that without ever thinking about constants, and would silently re-open the T1 class for patterns if this line were ever removed as "unused".

### ⛔ SIDE-FINDING — 50 STALE `.s` ARTIFACTS ARE **B2a's** DEBT, NOT g-cn2's
The mandatory step-4 regens moved **50 checked-in `.s` files** (SCRIP `89d569da` 43 feature · corpus `6faf02f4` 7 benchmark · corpus `65bf9aca` demo). **Attribute them to B2a, not to this rung.** Proof, not assertion: on a changed artifact the ORIGIN-HEAD binary and the fixed binary emit the byte-identical `.s` (`93a9a38e…` from both) while the file committed at `b69c63a5` was `60e748dd…` — **both** compilers disagree with the checked-in file, so it was stale before this seat existed; the corpus-wide `0 movers / 527` says the same. Every hunk is the match-β continuation cell (`lea rax, [rip + .Lx32_13]` + `mov qword ptr [rbp-48], rax`, comment `match_beta_cont`) — exactly what **B2a `d9f5cf24`** introduced. B2a landed on main WITHOUT its RULES-step-4 regens, so the artifacts have described a pre-B2a compiler ever since, and any seat reading them as current output was reading history. HQ owns B2/B2a — flagging, not touching.

### ⛔ ONE SEMANTIC QUESTION FOR LON — RECORDED, DELIBERATELY NOT RULED
Manual v3.7 **p.131: EVAL FAILS if evaluation of its argument fails**, leaving the reason in `&ERRTEXT`. SCRIP instead **ABORTS with 342** when a fragment reads an undeclared `&name`. Not changed here, for two reasons: (1) error→statement-failure IS the `&ERRLIMIT` mechanism SCRIP lacks — the same gap `kw_protected_write` is parked on (KW-5), so the two should move together; (2) 341/342 are SCRIP extensions with no oracle entry (the manual's table ends at **251 = "Keyword operand is not name of defined keyword"**, which is what stock `sbl` answers for this whole family), so their interaction with EVAL's failure semantics is a design call, not a repair. Today's behaviour is PINNED in `cn_t1_eval_undecl.err_sno` so KW-5 moves it deliberately.

### ⛔ NEXT SEAT — PICK UP EXACTLY HERE
**(1) LAW THIS ARGUES FOR:** a killswitch-byte-identity claim is a claim about the paths the instrument can SEE. Every tier that installs LOWERER-side static state (T1's literal table, T2's staged graphs, **and every tier CVA/T3 will add**) must mint its EVAL/CODE boundary witness **in the same commit as the tier** — the `.s` sweep structurally cannot cover it. **(2) T2 audit on `beauty_c`** — still owed from s151, unchanged: T2 cashes on the cn_t2 witness (DEFER 22→14) but no corpus program uses UDC, so the standard sweep is the wrong instrument. **(3) T3 / CVA** — needs an explicit Lon banner-ask before code (new file-scope state); note it inherits the boundary law in (1). **(4) CN-5 parked** pending `&`-in-GVA vs RTCC pricing; `NV_EXISTS_fn` at `keywords.c:360` is still the wrong 342 predicate (open since s148). **(5) Lon still to rule on worktree discipline** (s150 hazard); this seat used `/home/claude/SCRIP` + a throwaway baseline worktree and hit no collision.

### ⭐⭐⭐ CN-13 — CONST-GRAPH: EMIT-ONCE / LINK-MANY (Lon Q3, s162 in-chat: "keep a global offsets structure and deduce offsets WITHOUT inlining") — DESIGN NOTED FOR THE FUTURE, NOT YET A LIVE RUNG
**The gap it closes (measured s161/s162):** today's &PATTERN optimization is binary — inline → native boxes; not inlined → the runtime blob interpreter. The s142 entry cell and pat_static optimize the LOOKUP and the bookkeeping, never the match itself. CN-13 is the third road: **compile each declared constant's graph EXACTLY ONCE at a fixed label; every use site is a ~5-instruction linkage** (load r10=γ-wire/succ, r11=ω-wire/fail, `jmp CONST$Name_α`). The "global offsets structure" = a per-program directory: constant → {α label, spine/frame geometry, γ-retained-record contract}.
**Why the machine is already built for it (verified in-source, not asserted):** R10/R11 are the reserved LIVE γ/ω wires (register contract) · β-resume is ALREADY `jmp [rsp]` — spine records carry resume ADDRESSES, so a shared graph's suspensions interleave on the caller's spine and re-enter shared code with no new dispatch · ζ-SPINE is RSP-relative FORTH/LIFO → a shared graph is position-independent BY CONSTRUCTION; its interior offsets come from its own one-time zd_plan · the caller-side contract is the defer's existing one (s101: no compile-time-constant RSP distance across it; records self-describe) · defers already push a ζ-ACTIVATION frame — the shared graph's RBP slots plan once inside it · the s142 entry cell repoints from blob pointer to native-α and the site diet BECOMES the native call; stored-pattern blobs grow a "native leaf" op holding the same address (two homes, one code — the CVA story's code-side twin).
**What deletes when it lands:** the s161 TOP-LEVEL-ONLY depth limit and the sno_kw_chase cycle guard (a constant member inside an ALT arm becomes a LEAF call box — the nested-substitution m4 crash class is unreachable; recursion = the graph linking to itself); beauty's &White goes 1 graph + 42 thin sites instead of 42 copies. **Convergence:** the linkage protocol ("enter emitted code with return wires") is the SAME mechanism B1's by-name→SNOBOL-defined trampoline needs — one build, two walls.
**Boundary, stated so nobody oversells it:** a shared graph is still statement-regime boxes — the may-only-add-passes construct exclusions (FENCE/capture/BAL statement rungs) bound its CONTENT exactly as they bound inline. CN-13 fixes duplication, nesting, recursion, and the interpreter; the construct frontier advances on its own rungs.
**Ladder when activated:** (0) BB-CODEGEN DESIGN SET read first (templates/x86_asm.h work) + ARCH-SN4-CONSTANTS §CONST-GRAPH section; (1) leaf-only slice — one SPAN constant, one shared graph, two sites, witness = thin-site bytes + green both modes; (2) ALT-carrying constants (gate: cn_nest_alt_defer's defer count drops, output byte-identical); (3) recursion through the protocol (chase stack deletes); (4) blob native-leaf for value builds. Killswitch + off-arm byte-identity + blast ladder as always. Record: FINDING-2026-08-19-s161-CN12… carries the evidence trail this note compresses.

## ⛔⭐⭐⭐⭐⭐ LIVE CURSOR — 2026-08-19 s157 (HQ, Fable 5) — **T4 MINTED (Lon's constant-folding instinct, formalized): FOLD THE OPSYN — COMPILE-TIME OPERATOR BINDING KILLS B1 AT BEAUTY'S SITES WITHOUT TOUCHING THE RUNTIME. THE CRITICAL PATH RE-SEQUENCES: T4 → B2c → TAIL; CN-3c RIDES BEHIND T4.**

**T4 — OPSYN-FOLD (constant operator aliasing), the SN4-CONSTANTS worldview applied to OPERATOR BINDINGS:** `OPSYN('&','reduce',2)` with literal args, executed unconditionally in the preamble, is a COMPILE-TIME-VISIBLE constant binding — exactly the class Lon's declaration philosophy names. Fold it: rewrite `X & Y` call sites to DIRECT user-function calls (`reduce(X,Y)`) in the lowerer. The by-name runtime chain (B1's missing follow-on) is then BYPASSED at every folded site — no C→generated-code trampoline needed for beauty. Precedent machinery exists end-to-end: the lowerer already builds user-call nodes for `reduce(X,Y)` spelled directly; T2/CN-3 own the declaration-plumbing shape; killswitch + byte-identity + blast ladder standard. CONSERVATIVE ADMISSION (the T1 lesson): fold ONLY when the operator has exactly ONE OPSYN in the program, args literal, target a DEFINE'd function — anything dynamic keeps the runtime path (and thus TODAY'S m4 silent-empty, no worse). The general runtime follow-on (cures `$FN(X)` too) stays on the board as a SEAT brief (D-18 candidate) — T4 does not retire it, it just takes beauty off its critical path.
**Sequencing (supersedes s156's order):** (1) **T4** — HQ, next slice; gate = `b1_opsyn_binary_snodef` green in BOTH modes + beauty m4 parse movement (the wall's face is cheap to watch: statement count past `START`). (2) **B2c** carve-zeroing (m3). (3) Tail walls one per slice. CN-3c/T2b after T4 (B1 masks all constants payoffs until then — beauty_c Parse-Errors identically today).
**CN-9b (small, seat-ready):** `gen_beauty_c.py` predates CN-4 — beauty_c declares NOTHING (`&USER_DECLARED_CONSTANTS` absent), so T1/T2 cannot fire on it and my s151 payoff measurement ran against an undeclared namespace. Regen with the declaration line + re-measure (the T2-audit their s150 cursor asked for). AFTER T4.

**Honest speed answer to Lon ("how quickly?"):** T4 is one focused slice. If nothing else blocks the parse, beauty-m4 begins actually beautifying within **1–2 working sessions**; the full both-mode byte-identical fixed point is **plausibly ~3–5 sessions** — two named walls (T4-able B1, B2c) plus a tail the green boards bound but do not enumerate (grammar-in-anger, I/O association, byte-fidelity polish). The tail is where estimates die; the discipline (witness-per-wall, FINDING-per-slice) is what keeps each tail wall to ~a slice.

Fold at `sx_lower` TT_KEYWORD arm recurses into `sx_lower` on the stored literal — inherits `TT_ILIT/FLIT/QLIT` arms, no new emission path. ⛔ **`rt_kw_index()` guard is load-bearing, not belt-and-braces**: CN-3's "classification is free because no tier-2 keyword takes a pattern RHS" argument does NOT carry to T1 (integers/strings are exactly what `&ANCHOR`/`&CASE`/`&TRIM` take); without the guard, `&CASE = 1` would freeze into an immediate and silently break every program that relies on the keyword's mutability. `rt_kw_index` is block-own and killswitch-independent. `corpus/benchmarks/snobol4/timed/` — 12 programs + oracle-baked `.ref` + `NOISE-FLOOR.tsv`. Three scripts: `gen_timed_bench_snobol4.sh` (ONE AUTHORITY for the harness shape; the `.sno` are GENERATED) · `test_bench_snobol4_timed.sh` (sbl/m3/m4 throughput) · `bake_noise_floor_snobol4_timed.sh`. **Blast radius ZERO** — the legacy runner globs `"$B"/*.sno`, which does not descend into a subdirectory, so the 23 legacy programs and every artifact keyed to them are untouched. All 12 correctness-green in all 3 engines against oracle refs.

### ⛔⭐⭐⭐ THE HEADLINE — WHERE THE NEXT ORDER OF MAGNITUDE IS
m3:sbl, REPS=3, `SCRIP_NOHUGE=1`: `var_access` **6.21x** · `func_call` **4.88x** · `op_dispatch` **4.73x** · `arith_loop` **4.43x** · `fibonacci` **4.34x** · `string_concat` **3.04x** — then `pattern_bt` 0.98x · `eval_fixed` 0.72x · `string_pattern` 0.70x · `table_access` 0.51x · `array_sum` 0.43x · `string_manip` **0.38x**. **Every row above 3x is code the emitter produces; every row below 1x is a call into `src/runtime/`.** The emitted code is 4.3–6.2x the oracle; the C runtime it calls is 1.4–2.6x SLOWER. The next 10x is in the runtime library, not the codegen.

### ⛔⭐⭐ NEW DEFECT, MINTED AND ROUTED, NOT ROOT-CAUSED
**Transparent huge pages cost up to 2.26x on the allocating path** and are the entire source of that path's dispersion. `table_access_t` m3: 2,675/s → **6,042/s** with `SCRIP_NOHUGE=1`, cv 26.9% → 2.2%; `string_pattern_t` 1.26x, cv 22.8% → 0.6%; `array_sum_t` 1.16x, cv 34.6% → 2.9%. Scalar rows unaffected (~1.00x) — **only rows that allocate move**, so the signature is exact. The README's existing `table_churn` NOHUGE warning generalises to the whole allocating family and is a THROUGHPUT defect, not just measurement hygiene. Obvious suspect is the RBX frontier faulting 2 MiB pages it does not fill — **hypothesis, not measurement; do not assume it.**

### ⛔⛔ THE s148 ERROR REPEATED ITSELF HERE AND WAS CAUGHT — READ THIS BEFORE BUILDING ANY INSTRUMENT
The noise floor was first measured on ONE kernel (`arith_loop`, cv 1.3%) and was about to be written into the runner as a GLOBAL "~4%" constant. **It is false.** Per row the dispersion is **0.2%–34.6%**, and in the shipping arm `table_access_t` m3's min-detectable difference is **80.7%** — that row could not have seen a 2x regression while looking like a working benchmark. **The floor is a property of the (KERNEL, ENGINE, THP-arm) triple, never of the harness**; it is now baked per row and printed by the runner. Consequence, and the reason this matters: **a m4:m3 = 1.62x "MODE34 divergence" this seat reported in its first table was pure THP weather and is RETRACTED** — under the corrected instrument every row is 0.88–1.00x. An uncharacterized instrument's first plausible table is exactly the s33 "non-empty is not alive" false-signal class.

### ⛔ ALSO FOUND — THE LEGACY 23 HAVE A DEAD CORRECTNESS ORACLE
All 23 print a nondeterministic ms delta while all 23 sibling `.ref` hold a deterministic result (`iterations: 1000000`, `result: 60000012`, …), so **no `.ref` can ever match**; the runner's `grep -vi 'ms:'` filter strips only `roman.sno`'s line, the other 22 emit a bare number it does not catch. Also: all 23 set `&STLIMIT = 4294967295`, still paying the per-statement increment+check, where the manual (p.191) documents `-1` as the way to inhibit it *and* says it is faster. **NOT converted — Lon's call**, it touches the scorecard `benchmarks` suite (weight 10) and the `snobol4jvm`/`snobol4dotnet` loaders.

### ⛔ MANUAL DIVERGENCE ROUTED, NOT FIXED
`TIME()` (p.244) is **CPU time on Unix, excluding I/O wait**; SCRIP's `_TIME_` (`core.c:975`) uses `CLOCK_MONOTONIC` = **elapsed wall time**. Inert for a compute-bound deadline loop; live for any program that blocks on I/O, which would charge the wait to the budget where SPITBOL would not.

### ⛔ NEXT SEAT
(1) Root-cause the THP defect — 2.26x behind one env var. (2) Legacy-23 disposition (Lon's ruling). (3) `TIME()` wall-vs-CPU. (4) The timed family is deliberately **not** wired into `scorecard_snobol4.sh` — adding a suite reweights META and that is a scoring decision.

## ⛔⭐⭐⭐⭐⭐ LIVE CURSOR — 2026-08-19 s148 (web seat: Claude Opus 5, continuing D-3 KW-STATIC) — **KW-3b LANDED: THE WRITE SIDE IS NOW A SEALED STATIC INDEX TOO — THE ARMED ARM EMITS ZERO `SNO$KWSET` AND ZERO `rt_call_arr`. PLUS A LIVE ARMED-ARM FATAL FIXED (`&ERRTEXT` = string raised 208 AND TERMINATED). ⛔ TWO HOUSE SWEEPS NOT RUN — SEE "WHAT THIS SEAT DID NOT DO", THE RUNG IS GATED BUT NOT SWEPT.**
SCRIP `2796a66e` (KW-3b(a)) + `126ccda1` (KW-3b) · corpus `83548f3a`. Baseline this seat: SCRIP `6cc3aaee` (CN-3b), corpus `e327bbc0`.

### ⭐⭐⭐⭐⭐ WHAT LANDED — s148
- **KW-3b — `&KW = value` BY SEALED INDEX.** Both `lower_snobol4.c` TT_KEYWORD assignment sites (plain assign + statement-level pattern-replacement) mint a new kind instead of the by-name builtin call. MEASURED WIN, from the emitted `.s`: armed emits **zero `SNO$KWSET`, zero `rt_call_arr`** — a name-string pointer + `rt_call_arr@PLT` + `to_cstring` + a ~60-arm strcmp cascade, and THEN a second cascade inside `rt_keyword_write_snobol4`, all replaced by one rip-relative load + O(1) array write. Legacy arm keeps the by-name call verbatim.
- **KW-3b(a) — `&ERRTEXT` ACCEPTS A STRING.** `kwb_write_ent` raised **208 and TERMINATED** on `&ERRTEXT = 'text'` whenever armed (legacy merely swallowed it and read back null) — a hard blocker on ever flipping the default ON. Manual names the exception twice: Ch.14 Replacement field (*"must evaluate to an integer value (except for &ERRTEXT, which will accept a string)"*) and Ch.16 &ERRTEXT. ⛔ ORACLE-MEASURED, NOT INFERRED: `&ERRTEXT = 5` reads back **"5" / DATATYPE STRING**, so the entry accepts ANY value and stringifies it. Fixed in `kwb_write_ent` — the ONE body both `kwb_write` and `rt_kw_write_idx` execute — and deliberately **entry-specific, not `kind==KWB_STR`**: the other string entries are PROTECTED and `&ALPHABET = 'x'` must keep taking 208 (widening it takes `kw_protected_write.ref` oracle-wrong).

### ⛔⭐⭐⭐⭐ WHY A SEPARATE IR KIND — TWO MEASURED REASONS, NOT SYMMETRY (do not "collapse" this into `IR_KEYWORD_ASSIGN` for IR-REDUCE)
1. **NAMESPACE COLLISION, measured:** `TRACE` and `DUMP` are named by **BOTH** the SNOBOL4 keyword block and Icon's assign template, so the keyword NAME — the only key a shared template could dispatch on — cannot tell an Icon `&trace` from a SNOBOL4 `&TRACE`. And `sval`/`ival` are a **UNION** in `IR_lit_t`, so the lowerer cannot stash a resolved index beside the name to disambiguate.
2. **DIFFERENT FAILURE TOPOLOGY:** Icon's `&pos := v` genuinely FAILS and takes ω. A SNOBOL4 keyword write RAISES (208/209) and at the default `&ERRLIMIT` of 0 **TERMINATES** — manual Ch.16 `&ERRLIMIT` is explicit that only a NON-ZERO `&ERRLIMIT` decrements, suppresses the message and *"converts the error to statement failure"*, and SCRIP has no such mechanism (`core_runtime_error` exits). So the kind is **γ-only** and is deliberately ABSENT from the ω-push traversal lists — which is also where the `IR_CALL` it replaces sat, so traversal is identical to before.
Language stays implicit in PARSER/LOWER exactly as the read side does it: the lowerer picks the KIND, the emitter dispatches on the KIND, no language test anywhere.

### ⛔⭐⭐⭐⭐⭐ THE ZD TRAP — RECORDED SO THE NEXT SEAT DOES NOT RE-DERIVE IT
The READ sibling (`IR_KEYWORD_SNOBOL4`) is a **ZERO-OPERAND leaf**, so its `zd_nops` default of 0 is correct. This kind **CONSUMES** the assigned value, so it needs **`zd_nops == 1`**. Without that arm the ZD planner stages no `op_zread`, `ZOPQ(0,·)` resolves to **displacement 0 — THIS box's own result cell** — and the write reads back the descriptor it is about to store. Measured as `Error 208` on `&CASE = 1`, and **SILENT AT EMIT TIME** because displacement 0 is a perfectly legal address. Companion trap, this rung's FIRST failure: **operands are reached through `ZOPQ`/`ZOPD`, never `FRQ`** — `FRQ` is the legacy flat authority and is simply wrong in the ζ-cell regime (it read `rsp+48` where the operand sat at `rsp+16`).

### ⭐⭐⭐⭐⭐ KW-2 — WHAT LANDED
**The block homes each keyword AT THE CELL ITS CONSUMER ALREADY READS**, rather than taking a fresh copy. That is the design decision that matters: it RETIRES the spelled-twice disease instead of adding a third spelling, and it makes KW-3 a pure relocation (`rt_kw_bind()` is already the seam — reads and writes go THROUGH a bound pointer today).
- **A LIVE BUG FIXED, not just stale state: `&TRIM = 1` did nothing.** The write landed in keywords.c's `g_trim` while the input path tested core.c's `kw_trim`, so the keyword read back as 1 and never trimmed a line. Homing `&TRIM` at `kw_trim` is the entire fix. Witness `kw_trim_effect` (+`.dat` stdin).
- **CLASS B closed** — oracle-true initial values, NOT copied from the C initializers (`&TRIM`1 `&CASE`1 `&FULLSCAN`1 `&MAXLNGTH`16777216 `&STLIMIT`2147483647, + `&ABEND &INPUT &OUTPUT &PROFILE &COMPARE &ERRTYPE` which did not exist).
- **CLASS C closed** — `&UCASE`/`&LCASE` are STRING; `kw_read` left untouched so Icon keeps its csets.
- **CLASS A closed both halves** — read (`NV_GET_fn`) and write (`NV_SET_fn`) families gated TOGETHER so they cannot disagree. Gated, not deleted; KW-4 deletes after measuring.
- **CLASS D enforced** — 209 protected / 208 non-integer, **in the oracle's order** (the value test fires BEFORE the protected test; swap them and `&ALPHABET = 'x'` goes oracle-wrong).

### ⛔ THE REGRESSION THE 10-ROW GATE MISSED AND THE BY-SET A/B CAUGHT
Seeding runs **lazily at first keyword touch**, not at program start — so initializing PROTECTED entries **rewound the live `&STNO`/`&STCOUNT` counters**, taking `crosscheck/keywords/082_keyword_stcount` PASS→FAIL. Fixed: protected repositories are NEVER initialized; they own their value and the block only reads it. **Lesson for KW-3: a small witness gate does not substitute for the by-set A/B.** Both were needed; only the second saw this.

### ⛔ STILL RED — BOTH ROUTED, NEITHER TAKEABLE ON THIS SEAT
- **`kw_protected_write`** needs the **`&ERRLIMIT` → statement-failure** mechanism, which **SCRIP does not have at all** — `kw_errlimit` is a dead static, nothing converts an error to statement failure, and the error-report block format differs from the oracle's. That is its own rung (call it KW-5 / ERRLIMIT), independent of KW-STATIC.
- **`kw_bare_shadow`'s last two lines** are `DATATYPE(<unset variable>)` = `NULL` vs oracle `STRING` — **HQ's B1**, handed over, not taken (below).

### ⭐⭐⭐⭐ HANDOFF TO HQ — B1 IS LOCALIZED, AND DATATYPE IS INNOCENT
Found while clearing the bare-name class; witness `corpus/probe/kw/kw_unset_datatype.{sno,ref}`. Four lines separate the defect from its neighbours: a **null literal**, an **assigned null**, and a **non-null string** are ALL correct in SCRIP today; **only an UNSET VARIABLE differs** (oracle STRING, SCRIP NULL). So the s145 suspect "DATATYPE(null) = NULL" is more precisely: **DATATYPE is innocent — the wrong datum is the descriptor an unset variable yields**, which carries a distinct NULL tag instead of being the null string manual p.24 gives every fresh variable. **Identical on both killswitch arms**, confirming it is not keyword-related. ShiftReduce.inc calling DATATYPE ×2 remains the reason this reaches beauty.

### ⛔ NEXT SEAT — PICK UP EXACTLY HERE
**KW-3** — emit the block into the program's `.data` and bind it via `rt_kw_bind()` in the main prologue; retarget `bb_keyword_snobol4` reads to `[rip+disp]`; give `&KW =` a real template instead of the `SNO$KWSET` by-name builtin call (measured cost today: a read emits a pointer to the keyword's NAME STRING + a ~60-arm strcmp cascade; a write has no template at all). BOTH-MEDIUM, TEMPLATE-ONLY. Then **KW-4** — delete the gated bare-name family, the `kw_*` statics, the `SCRIP_SEED_NAMES` ALPHABET bridge, and `bb_match_advance`/`IR_MATCH_ADVANCE` (dead; last template reference to `kw_anchor`); gates + md5 blast radius. ⚠ **Re-run the by-set A/B, not just the witness gate, after every rung.** ⚠ **Flipping the killswitch default to ON is its own gated step** — Class B changes `&TRIM`/`&FULLSCAN` behaviour for every program, and the 802-row A/B is the evidence required.

- **SPELLED-TWICE DISEASE (the structural find).** Every integer keyword is spelled TWICE: `g_*` in keywords.c vs `kw_*` in core.c. **`&MAXLNGTH` disagrees between them (5000000 vs 524288).** Which family you reach depends on how you spell the read: `&ANCHOR`→`g_anchor`, bare `ANCHOR`→`kw_anchor`. `kw_*` is written only via `ASGNIC_fn`, whose sole caller is driver_call.c:238 ⇒ in a compiled program the `kw_*` family is **write-dead and read-live** — stale zeros feeding `NV_GET_fn`.

The live retry bump is inlined in `bb_match_begin`'s β and correctly reads `rt_anchor_g` (verified in emitted `.s`). Delete both; it is the last template-layer reference to `kw_anchor`.
- **THE COST KW-STATIC REMOVES, measured from emitted `.s`:** a `&KW` READ emits a pointer to the keyword's NAME STRING + `call rt_keyword_read_snobol4@PLT` → lowercase into a 64B buffer → ~60-arm strcmp cascade → possible `NV_GET_fn` hash lookup. A `&KW =` WRITE has no template at all — it lowers to a BY-NAME BUILTIN CALL, `.string "SNO$KWSET"` → `rt_call_arr@PLT` → `to_cstring` → a second cascade.

### ⭐⭐⭐⭐⭐ SN4-CONSTANTS — USER-DECLARED `&` CONSTANTS, THE MASSIVE EXTENSION (**design home: `ARCH-SN4-CONSTANTS.md`**; Lon in-chat 2026-08-19, verbatim in substance: *"We are adding CONSTANTS. And the syntax is already there '&' ampersand. Of course we keep the already existing &keyword constants, and we keep the FEW keyword VARIABLES (rarely used and usually problematic like &ANCHOR and &STLIMIT). But the user can do a ONE TIME assignment which will be guaranteed never to be re-assigned. We probably need a BIT in the DT_T so at runtime we can check for this. We must be able to SAY, they are CONSTANT GUARANTEED. So patterns that are usually 99% constant can be DECLARED and the compiler can OPTIMIZE."*)
**THE NAMESPACE (three tiers, resolution in this order):** (1) protected keywords — constants already (`&ALPHABET &ARB &BAL &FENCE &ABORT &FAIL &REM &SUCCEED &UCASE &LCASE &STCOUNT &STNO …`), unchanged; (2) the CLOSED unprotected-keyword list — the few true keyword VARIABLES (`&ANCHOR &TRIM &STLIMIT &MAXLNGTH &FULLSCAN &DUMP &ERRLIMIT &CODE &CASE &FTRACE &TRACE &ABEND &COMPARE &PROFILE &ERRTEXT &ERRTYPE`), still assignable per the manual; (3) **everything else `&name` = a USER CONSTANT** — one-time assignment, immutable forever after. Bare `name` stays an ordinary variable in its own namespace; SCRIP case-sensitivity applies.
**SEMANTICS (defaults chosen by HQ, Lon may veto any):** `&X = expr` executes once and seals; a SECOND textual definition of the same `&X` = COMPILE error; dynamic re-execution of the one definition statement or any other assignment reaching a sealed cell = LOUD runtime error (new error number, manual-style text "constant re-assignment"); READING an `&X` that has no textual definition = COMPILE error; reading one whose definition has not yet EXECUTED = LOUD runtime error (silent null would defeat "GUARANTEED"). `OPSYN`/indirect `$('&X')`/`FIELD`-style aliasing cannot bypass the seal (enforced at the cell, not the spelling).
**THE GUARANTEE MECHANISM (two layers):** (a) the BIT — Lon's ask: a constancy bit checkable at runtime; CN-1 decides its home after reading `descr.h` + `GOAL-DESCR-TAG-ENCODING.md` (candidates: a DESCR tag bit so constancy travels WITH the value, and/or an NV-cell `is_const` beside the existing `is_gva` — the cell bit guards writes, the value bit lets the optimizer trust what flows); (b) the PAGE — constants live in the KW-STATIC emitted block's sibling CONSTANTS segment, `mprotect(PROT_READ)` after program init: re-assignment isn't just checked, it FAULTS. Hardware is the auditor of last resort.
**THE OPTIMIZER PAYOFF (why this is MASSIVE):** T1 scalar constants → immediates/rodata in emitted code, zero NV/GVA reads. T2 **CONSTANT PATTERNS** → `pat_static` becomes a DECLARED GUARANTEE instead of a conservative inference: the pattern graph stages at compile time, and every `*P`-defer / stored-ARBNO resume record / re-evaluation guard on a declared-constant target is BYPASSED — the s121–s145 defer-machinery pain class shrinks to genuinely-dynamic patterns only. T3 constant strings/tables → rodata, no GC scan, no write barriers. (KW-STATIC synergy is exact: same block mechanism, same bind call, one extra RO segment.)
**CN-5 — PARSER-ACTION COMPILER PRIMITIVES (Lon's ruling 2026-08-19 late, verbatim in substance: *"nPush will be CONSTANT forever, never ALLOWED to be changed. We'll probably pull it inside the compiler since it has the two-level semantic problem at runtime which it most likely will not have at compile time. So nPush/nPop FAMILY is a compiler primitive. We'll eventually remove the *.inc file most likely."* — SUPERSEDES the earlier precedence question: BUILTIN WINS, ALWAYS; a user DEFINE of these names is an error against a sealed compiler name):** the family (`nPush nInc nDec nPop nTop`) lowers AT COMPILE TIME to dedicated zero-width pattern boxes — counter side-effect + backtrack-correct undo via the four ports — with NO DEFINE, NO `'' . *Fn()` thunk, NO EXPR$ blob, NO suspension record. THE POINT (Lon's "two-level semantic problem"): the runtime encoding of these actions is a pattern-embedded deferred CALL — exactly the thunk/record machinery wall B2 inhabits — and it vanishes at compile time. END STATE: `semantic.inc`/`counter.inc` retire; beauty_c's wave-N drops them and rides the primitives — a SECOND independent road to beauty-m3 beside the B2 engine fix (which stays owed regardless: the runtime shape is legal SNOBOL4 for user code). Reference semantics still extracted from Lon's library FIRST (the primitives must reproduce observed oracle behavior of the .inc originals, incl. unwind-on-backtrack which the .inc versions could never do — that part is a designed IMPROVEMENT, to be measured against grammar correctness not byte-echo).
**RUNGS:** CN-0 spec vs manual ch.16 keyword lists + error-number allocation · CN-1 the bit + cell decision (`descr.h`, `GOAL-DESCR-TAG-ENCODING.md`) + parser/lower plumbing (`&name` LHS with non-keyword name = const-def; TT_KEYWORD read path resolves tier 3) · CN-2 ✅ LANDED AT HQ s145 (defer arm + canonical `&Name` NV key + seal 341/342 with offending-name messages + NV_EXISTS + the is_const bit; witnesses green; 529/529 byte-identity BOTH arms; beauty_c runs BYTE-EQUAL to classic beauty on the same engine — zero constants-regression, both stop at wall B2) (⛔ extension programs ORACLE_FAIL by construction — pin `.ref`s, the scorecard's pin-only path covers this) · CN-3 constant PATTERNS: guarantee-propagation into `pat_static`, defer-site bypass, measured on a beauty-shaped `ARBNO(*Command)` grammar mimic · CN-4 the **CVA** (Constant Variable Area, Lon 2026-08-19 — design: `ARCH-SN4-CONSTANTS.md` § CVA): sealed sibling of the KW-STATIC block / mmap arena with page-granular progressive sealing, + immediates, killswitch + full gates.

**β cannot trust the ambient `rbp`.** Defer suspends at γ still holding its frame; by the time β resumes, other activations (MATCH_BEGIN, sibling defer, next ARBNO instance) may have pushed their own. `rbp` callee-saved protects it across C crossings, not across a *suspension*.

### ⭐⭐⭐ WHY THE PROBE FOUND NOTHING ON THE CORPSE — STRUCTURAL, NOT A BUG
It checks the **BASE** (`RSP0−rsp` vs the lattice) and never the **DISPLACEMENT**. An operand banked at one offset and read at another is sound in rsp at both ends. That is the `deferclob` class verbatim (s133: *"BOTH DEPTHS ARE KNOWABLE; they disagree about WHAT IS STORED WHERE"*). The check that would catch it records `(slot_id, effective_address)` at the write and asserts it recomputes at the read — which needs both sites routed through ONE accessor to have a `slot_id` at all. **Distributing `zone_ref` is therefore not cleanup ahead of the real work; it is the precondition for the check that can find the class.**

## ⭐⭐⭐⭐ BENCHMARK-PROPER — TIMED LOOPS BESIDE ITERATION LOOPS (Lon 2026-08-19 in-chat, verbatim in substance: *"get benchmarks working as TIMED loops versus ITERATION loops … industry standard technique of time limiting versus count limiting"* — the 3rd concurrent GOAL-SNOBOL4-100 session)
**THE MODEL:** every benchmark splits into KERNEL (the work, an `-INCLUDE`-able body) + two thin DRIVERS: the **iteration driver** (count-limited, fixed N — DETERMINISTIC output, stays a correctness row and the cross-engine comparability anchor) and the **timed driver** (industry shape: WARMUP window, then run the kernel until `TIME() - t0 >= MEASURE_MS`, count completed iterations, report THROUGHPUT `ips:` — iterations/second). Timing lines are NEVER correctness: the scorecard's `norm=ms` precedent extends to `iters:`/`ips:` lines (rewritten on both sides before diff). The oracle runs the SAME drivers — that makes the throughput table the direct instrument for PLAN.md's product claim ("Ten times faster"): ips(SCRIP m3/m4) ÷ ips(sbl), per kernel.
**LAWS THAT BIND:** O2-DIRECTED-ONLY (every number labeled with its RT_OPT level) · median-of-3 minimum, min/median reported, single runs flagged as noise · `&STLIMIT` pinned high in timed drivers (a statement limit silently truncates a timed loop — measure the default, then set it explicitly) · TIME() semantics PINNED on both engines before any number is quoted (granularity, cpu-vs-wall, manual §TIME vs measured binary — the manual-is-stale law applies) · perf is a LANE BESIDE the META score, never inside it (weights untouched).
**RUNGS:** **BM-0** TIME()/&STLIMIT semantics pinned by oracle probe both engines + survey of the 23 existing benchmarks (which are count-limited today; s91: 12/15 of 23 scoreable) · **BM-1** the harness convention — `bench.inc` timing scaffold (warmup/measure/emit), the two-driver split, scorecard norm extension · **BM-2** convert the 23 (kernel extraction; iteration drivers stay correctness rows; timed drivers added; oracle-verified) · **BM-3** `scripts/bench_report.sh` — the throughput table sbl vs m3 vs m4 with ratio column, median-of-3, environment header (RT_OPT, host) · **BM-4** scorecard PERF lane wiring + the standing "10×" watermark tracked per session.
## ⭐⭐⭐⭐ WIRE-ORDER — THE γ/ω PAIR PROTOCOL (Lon 2026-08-19 in-chat, verbatim in substance: *"R10 (GAMMA) and R11 (OMEGA) stored on the stack together as the TWO POSSIBLE return locations. I LOVE THAT... ensure that EVERYWHERE the protocol ALWAYS pushes those in the EXACT order. And also store them in FRAMES in a consistent order for ALL FRAMES... an order of SAVING which I also want to be CONSISTENT EVERYWHERE."*)
**VERIFIED BY CENSUS (HQ, same day, zero deviants):** ONE memory order everywhere measured — **γ(r10) at the LOW address (+0), ω(r11) at +8** — across frame stores (24/24 pair-sites `FRQ(op_off)/FRQ(op_off+8)`), stack pushes (4/4), and the RTCC call-spill slots (`rtccb+56`/`+64`, ascending). ⛔ **THE PUSH-SEQUENCE TRAP, now law:** to produce γ@+0 on a downward stack the SEQUENCE is `push r11; push r10` — sequence is the inverse of layout BY NECESSITY. Any new site pushes ω first, stores γ low. Gate (D-14 mints it): greps for `push r10`-before-`r11` and for `FRQ(op_off)` paired with r11 == 0 forever.
**THE ALWAYS-LIVE QUESTION (Lon, same message: *"should we keep R10 and R11 always LIVE for every BB in every landing, since of course those two are saved for every activation?"*):** the register contract already declares R10/R11 LIVE-never-scratch, and RTCC already spills/restores the pair around C calls — the HOLES are paths that skip the restore (the s44 WIRE-RESTORE bug class: "without this, x86_omega reads r11 == this node's own dead L(5)"). Lon's proposal upgrades the contract to an ENFORCED INVARIANT: r10/r11 equal the running BB's staged wires at EVERY port landing. HQ position: YES as instrument-then-repair — mint a ζ-SM-style WIRE CANARY (compare r10/r11 at each port against the staged wires under an env gate), census the violations corpus-wide, repair the holes the census names, then the canary becomes the standing gate. Cost of blanket restore-at-landing is measured by the census before any blanket decision.
## ⛔⭐⭐⭐⭐⭐ FOUR PORT NAMES ONLY — NEVER NAME A BB PORT ANYTHING BUT α β γ ω (Lon in-chat, s133)

**A BB HAS EXACTLY FOUR PORTS: `α` fresh entry · `β` resume/backtrack-retry · `γ` success/advance · `ω` fail.** RULES.md already says *FOUR PORTS = FOUR GREEK NAMES ALWAYS* — this is that law's MEASUREMENT-SIDE corollary, earned because a session violated it while believing it was obeying it.

⛔ **THE INTERNAL GLUE OF A BOX IS NOT A PORT.** The n-ary ALT/ARBNO success- and fail-glue defines (`na_s`/`na_f` at `emit.cpp:1391-1394`, whose edge tags are spelled `0xcf83`/`0xcf86` in `IR_ref_t.sz` beside β's `0xceb2`) are LABELS A BOX DEFINES FOR ITS OWN INTERNAL DISPATCH. They are not entry ports, no template treats them as ports, and nothing may ever present them in a port column, a port grid, or a port-to-port transition table.

⛔ **CONVICTION (s133):** a seat censused 16,082 edges and reported a SEVEN-ROW grid with `γ→σ`, `γ→φ` and `ω→φ` as rows beside `γ→α` and `ω→β`. Lon rejected it on sight — *"I did not like your grid because I see more than FOUR ports. So that is invalid."* The grid was arithmetically correct and architecturally false: it manufactured a five- and six-port machine that does not exist, and it buried the one fact that mattered (γ never lands at β) among rows describing glue.

⭐ **HOW TO SAY IT INSTEAD.** When an edge lands on a box's internal glue, name it in four-port terms and say whose box it re-enters: *"the body's γ returns into the enclosing ARBNO box, which then either re-enters the body's α for another iteration or takes ARBNO's own γ to advance"* — NEVER *"γ→σ"*. The tag bytes stay in the code as an implementation detail of the edge encoding; they never reach a document, a census header, a finding, or a sentence.

**ENFORCEMENT:** any port table, census output, or FINDING naming a fifth port is REJECTED. A census that must distinguish glue edges reports them as *"returns into <box>"* rows, not as port rows.
